### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A cylinder with adiabatic walls is closed at both ends and is divided into two compartments by a frictionless adiabatic piston. Ideal gas is filled in both (left and right) the compartments at same $P, V, T$. Heating is started from left side until pressure changes to $\dfrac{27P}{8}$. If initial volume of each compartment was $9$ litres then the final volume in right-hand side compartment is __________ litres. (for this ideal gas $C_P/C_V = 1.5$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3$
- **(B)** $4$
- **(C)** $14$
- **(D)** $9$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The right compartment is enclosed by adiabatic walls and an adiabatic piston, and no heat is supplied to it. Therefore, the gas in the right compartment undergoes an adiabatic compression.

Since the piston is frictionless, the pressure in the right compartment is always equal to the pressure in the left compartment. The final pressure in both compartments is $P_f = \dfrac{27P}{8}$.

For the adiabatic process in the right compartment, the equation is:
$P_i V_i^\gamma = P_f V_f^\gamma$

Given $P_i = P$, $V_i = 9$ L, $P_f = \dfrac{27P}{8}$, and $\gamma = \dfrac{C_P}{C_V} = 1.5 = \dfrac{3}{2}$. Substituting these values:
$P(9)^{3/2} = \left(\dfrac{27P}{8}\right) V_f^{3/2}$

Since $9^{3/2} = (3^2)^{3/2} = 3^3 = 27$, the equation becomes:
$27P = \dfrac{27P}{8} V_f^{3/2}$

Dividing both sides by $27P$:
$1 = \dfrac{1}{8} V_f^{3/2}$

$V_f^{3/2} = 8$

Taking the power of $2/3$ on both sides:
$V_f = (8)^{2/3} = (2^3)^{2/3} = 2^2 = 4$ litres.

Answer: $4$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A certain gas is isothermally compressed to $\left(\dfrac{1}{3}\right)^{rd}$ of its initial volume ($V_o = 3$ litre) by applying required pressure. If the bulk modulus of the gas is $3 \times 10^5$ N/m$^2$, the magnitude of work done on the gas is _______ J.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **989**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an isothermal process, the isothermal bulk modulus equals the gas pressure:
$B_T = -V\dfrac{dP}{dV}$

Since $PV = \text{constant}$, differentiating gives $P\,dV + V\,dP = 0$, so $\dfrac{dP}{dV} = -\dfrac{P}{V}$, which yields $B_T = P$.

Therefore, the initial pressure is:
$P_o = 3 \times 10^5\text{ N/m}^2$

Volumes:
$V_o = 3\text{ L} = 3 \times 10^{-3}\text{ m}^3$
$V_f = \dfrac{1}{3}V_o = 10^{-3}\text{ m}^3$

Work done on the gas during an isothermal compression:
$W_{\text{on}} = -W_{\text{by}} = -P_o V_o \ln\left(\dfrac{V_f}{V_o}\right) = P_o V_o \ln\left(\dfrac{V_o}{V_f}\right)$

$W_{\text{on}} = (3 \times 10^5)(3 \times 10^{-3})\ln 3$

$W_{\text{on}} = 900 \ln 3\text{ J}$

Using $\ln 3 \approx 1.0986$:
$W_{\text{on}} \approx 900 \times 1.0986 \approx 988.74\text{ J}$

Rounded to the nearest integer:
$W_{\text{on}} \approx 989\text{ J}$

Hence, the work done on the gas is approximately $989\text{ J}$.

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two closed vessels of same volume are joined through a narrow tube and both vessels are filled with air of pressure $90$ kPa and temperature $400$ K. Keeping the temperature of one vessel constant at $400$ K the second vessel temperature is raised to $500$ K. The final pressure in the vessels is _______ kPa.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $100$
- **(B)** $120$
- **(C)** $90$
- **(D)** $105$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $100$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the volume of each vessel be $V$.

Initially, both vessels are at pressure $P_1 = 90$ kPa and temperature $T_1 = 400$ K.

Total number of moles initially is given by:

$n_i = \dfrac{P_1 V}{R T_1} + \dfrac{P_1 V}{R T_1} = \dfrac{2 P_1 V}{R T_1}$

Finally, the temperature of the first vessel is $T_1 = 400$ K and the second vessel is $T_2 = 500$ K. Let the final pressure be $P_f$.

Total number of moles finally is given by:

$n_f = \dfrac{P_f V}{R T_1} + \dfrac{P_f V}{R T_2}$

Since the total number of moles remains constant, $n_i = n_f$:

$\dfrac{2 P_1 V}{R T_1} = \dfrac{P_f V}{R T_1} + \dfrac{P_f V}{R T_2}$

Substituting the given values:

$\dfrac{2 \times 90}{400} = \dfrac{P_f}{400} + \dfrac{P_f}{500}$

$\dfrac{180}{400} = P_f \left( \dfrac{1}{400} + \dfrac{1}{500} \right)$

$\dfrac{180}{400} = P_f \left( \dfrac{5 + 4}{2000} \right)$

$\dfrac{180}{400} = P_f \left( \dfrac{9}{2000} \right)$

$P_f = \dfrac{180 \times 2000}{400 \times 9}$

$P_f = 20 \times 5 = 100$ kPa

Answer: $100$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An ideal gas at pressure $P$ and temperature $T$ is expanding such that $PT^3 = $ constant. The coefficient of volume expansion of the gas is _______.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{2}{T}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{1}{T}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{4}{T}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{3}{T}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\dfrac{4}{T}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given the process equation for the ideal gas:

$PT^3 = \text{constant}$

From the ideal gas equation, $PV = nRT$, which gives $P = \dfrac{nRT}{V}$.

Substituting $P$ into the process equation:

$\left(\dfrac{nRT}{V}\right) T^3 = \text{constant}$

$\dfrac{T^4}{V} = \text{constant}$

$V = k T^4$

Differentiating both sides with respect to temperature $T$:

$\dfrac{dV}{dT} = 4k T^3$

The coefficient of volume expansion $\gamma$ is defined as:

$\gamma = \dfrac{1}{V} \dfrac{dV}{dT}$

Substituting the values of $V$ and $\dfrac{dV}{dT}$:

$\gamma = \dfrac{1}{k T^4} (4k T^3) = \dfrac{4}{T}$

Answer: $\dfrac{4}{T}$

</details>

---

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An ideal gas undergoes a process maintaining relation between pressure $(P)$ and volume $(V)$ as $P = P_o\left(1 + \left(\dfrac{V_o}{V}\right)^2\right)^{-1}$, where $P_o$ and $V_o$ are constants. If two samples $A$ and $B$ (two moles each) with initial volumes $V_o$ and $3V_o$ respectively undergo above mentioned process and attain same pressure, then the difference at the temperatures of these samples, $T_B - T_A$ is _____. ($R = $ gas constant)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{9P_o V_o}{8R}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{11P_o V_o}{10R}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{7P_o V_o}{6R}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{13P_o V_o}{11R}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\dfrac{11P_o V_o}{10R}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given relation between pressure and volume is:

$P = P_o\left(1 + \left(\dfrac{V_o}{V}\right)^2\right)^{-1}$

For sample A, the initial volume is $V_A = V_o$. Substituting this into the given relation, the initial pressure $P_A$ is:

$P_A = P_o\left(1 + \left(\dfrac{V_o}{V_o}\right)^2\right)^{-1} = \dfrac{P_o}{2}$

Using the ideal gas equation $PV = nRT$ for $n = 2$ moles, the initial temperature $T_A$ is:

$T_A = \dfrac{P_A V_A}{nR} = \dfrac{\left(\dfrac{P_o}{2}\right) V_o}{2R} = \dfrac{P_o V_o}{4R}$

For sample B, the initial volume is $V_B = 3V_o$. Substituting this into the relation, the initial pressure $P_B$ is:

$P_B = P_o\left(1 + \left(\dfrac{V_o}{3V_o}\right)^2\right)^{-1} = P_o\left(1 + \dfrac{1}{9}\right)^{-1} = \dfrac{9P_o}{10}$

The initial temperature $T_B$ for $n = 2$ moles is:

$T_B = \dfrac{P_B V_B}{nR} = \dfrac{\left(\dfrac{9P_o}{10}\right) (3V_o)}{2R} = \dfrac{27P_o V_o}{20R}$

The difference in the initial temperatures of the two samples is:

$T_B - T_A = \dfrac{27P_o V_o}{20R} - \dfrac{P_o V_o}{4R}$

$T_B - T_A = \dfrac{27P_o V_o - 5P_o V_o}{20R} = \dfrac{22P_o V_o}{20R} = \dfrac{11P_o V_o}{10R}$

Answer: $\dfrac{11P_o V_o}{10R}$

</details>

---

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A vessel contains $0.15$ m$^3$ of a gas at pressure $8$ bar and temperature $140°$C with $c_p = 3R$ and $c_v = 2R$. It is expanded adiabatically till pressure falls to $1$ bar. The work done during this process is _______ kJ. ($R$ is gas constant)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **120**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $P_1 = 8$ bar $= 8 \times 10^5$ Pa, $V_1 = 0.15$ m$^3$, and $P_2 = 1$ bar $= 10^5$ Pa.

The adiabatic index $\gamma$ is given by:

$\gamma = \dfrac{c_p}{c_v} = \dfrac{3R}{2R} = 1.5$

For an adiabatic process, $P_1 V_1^\gamma = P_2 V_2^\gamma$.

$V_2 = V_1 \left( \dfrac{P_1}{P_2} \right)^{\dfrac{1}{\gamma}}$

$V_2 = 0.15 \times \left( \dfrac{8}{1} \right)^{\dfrac{1}{1.5}} = 0.15 \times 8^{\dfrac{2}{3}} = 0.15 \times 4 = 0.6$ m$^3$

The work done during the adiabatic expansion is:

$W = \dfrac{P_1 V_1 - P_2 V_2}{\gamma - 1}$

$W = \dfrac{8 \times 10^5 \times 0.15 - 1 \times 10^5 \times 0.6}{1.5 - 1}$

$W = \dfrac{1.2 \times 10^5 - 0.6 \times 10^5}{0.5}$

$W = \dfrac{0.6 \times 10^5}{0.5} = 1.2 \times 10^5$ J

$W = 120$ kJ

Answer: $120$

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A thermodynamic system is taken through the cyclic process $A B C$ as shown in the figure. The total work done by the system during the cycle $A B C$ is $\_\_\_\_$ J. 
 <img style="width:250px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/28S2_q_46_1.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **300**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the P-V diagram: A(2,100), B(5,300), C(5,100) in Pa and m³ units.
Process A→B is linear. Equation: $P = 100 + \frac{200}{3}(V-2)$
$W_{AB} = \int_2^5 \frac{200V - 100}{3} dV = \frac{1}{3}[100V^2 - 100V]_2^5 = \frac{1800}{3} = 600$ J
Process B→C is isobaric: $W_{BC} = 300(5-5) = 0$ J
Process C→A is isobaric: $W_{CA} = 100(2-5) = -300$ J
Total work done by system: $W = 600 + 0 - 300 = 300$ J

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

In the following $p-V$ diagram the equation of state along the curved path is given by $(V-2)^{2}=4 a p$ where $a$ is a constant. The total work done in the closed path is 
 <img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/28S1_q_42_1_v2.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+\frac{1}{3 a}$
- **(B)** $-\frac{1}{3 a}$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{2 a}$
- **(D)** $-\frac{1}{a}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-\frac{1}{3 a}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the equation of state $(V-2)^2 = 4ap$, we get $p = \frac{(V-2)^2}{4a}$.
At V = 1: $p = \frac{1}{4a}$ and at V = 3: $p = \frac{1}{4a}$.
The curved path A→B→C follows the given equation, while C→A is a straight line at constant pressure.
Work along the curved path:
$W_{ABC} = \int_1^3 \frac{(V-2)^2}{4a} dV = \frac{1}{4a}\left[\frac{(V-2)^3}{3}\right]_1^3 = \frac{1}{4a} \cdot \frac{2}{3} = \frac{1}{6a}$.
Work along C→A at constant pressure:
$W_{CA} = \frac{1}{4a}(1-3) = -\frac{1}{2a}$.
Total work: $W_{total} = \frac{1}{6a} - \frac{1}{2a} = -\frac{1}{3a}$.

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

When 300 J of heat given to an ideal gas with $C_{p}=\frac{7}{2} R$ its temperature raises from $20^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ to $50^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ keeping its volume constant. If n is the number of moles of the gas, then what is the value of $100n$? $(\mathrm{R}=8.314 \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{mol}. \mathrm{K})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **48**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given: $C_P = \frac{7}{2}R$, so $C_V = C_P - R = \frac{5}{2}R$
$\Delta Q = nC_V \Delta T$ (constant volume)
$300 = n \times \frac{5}{2} \times 8.314 \times 30$
$n = 0.48$ mol
$100n = 48$

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

One mole of an ideal diatomic gas expands from volume $V$ to 2 V isothermally at a temperature $27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ and does $W$ joule of work. If the gas undergoes same magnitude of expansion adiabatically from $27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ doing the same amount of work $W$, then its final temperature will be (close to) $\_\_\_\_$ ${ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. 
  $\left(\log _{\mathrm{e}} 2=0.693\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** -30
- **(B)** -189
- **(C)** -117
- **(D)** -56

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) -56**

**Detailed Solution:**

For isothermal expansion of 1 mole diatomic gas from V to 2V at 300 K:
$W = RT\ln 2 = R(300)\ln 2$ 
For adiabatic expansion with same volume change and same work output:
$W = nC_V\Delta T = C_V(T_i - T_f)$ 
For diatomic gas, $C_V = \frac{5}{2}R$. Setting work equal:
$R(300)\ln 2 = \frac{5}{2}R(300 - T_f)$ 
Simplifying:
$300\ln 2 = 2.5(300 - T_f)$, so $300(0.693) = 2.5(300 - T_f)$ 
$207.9 = 750 - 2.5T_f$, giving $T_f = 216.84$ K $\approx$ -56°C.

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

An insulated cylinder of volume $60 \mathrm{~cm}^{3}$ is filled with a gas at $27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ and 2 atmospheric pressure. Then the gas is compressed making the final volume as $20 \mathrm{~cm}^{3}$ while allowing the temperature to rise to $77^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. The final pressure is $\_\_\_\_$ atmospheric pressure.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **7**

**Detailed Solution:**

$PV = nRT$
$\dfrac{P_1 V_1}{RT_1} = \dfrac{P_2 V_2}{RT_2}$
$\dfrac{2 \times 10^5 \times 60}{300} = \dfrac{P_2 \times 20}{350}$
$P_2 = \dfrac{2 \times 10^5 \times 7 \times 3}{6}$
$P_2 = 7 \times 10^5 = 7 \text{ atm}$

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A diatomic gas $(\gamma=1.4)$ does 100 J of work when it is expanded isobarically. Then the heat given to the gas $\_\_\_\_$ J.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **350**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an isobaric process, the first law gives: $Q = \Delta U + W$.
For a diatomic gas with $\gamma = 1.4$, we have $C_V = \frac{5}{2}R$ and $C_P = \frac{7}{2}R$.
Since $W = nR\Delta T = 100$ J for isobaric expansion, the change in internal energy is: $\Delta U = nC_V\Delta T = \frac{5}{2}nR\Delta T = \frac{5}{2} \times 100 = 250$ J.
Therefore, $Q = 250 + 100 = 350$ J.

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2025 (8 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A monoatomic gas having $\gamma=\frac{5}{3}$ is stored in a thermally insulated container and the gas is suddenly compressed to $\left(\frac{1}{8}\right)^{\text {th }}$ of its initial volume. The ratio of final pressure and initial pressure is: ( $\gamma$ is the ratio of specific heats of the gas at constant pressure and at constant volume)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 16
- **(B)** 40
- **(C)** 32
- **(D)** 28

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 32**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & P_i V_i^\gamma=P_f V_f^\gamma \\ & \frac{P_f}{P_i}=\left(\frac{V_i}{V_f}\right)^\gamma=(8)^{5 / 3} \\ & \frac{P_f}{P_i}=32\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Match List-I with List-II.
$\begin{array}{|l|l|l|l|} \hline & \text{List-I} & & \text{List-II} \\ \hline \text{(A)} & \text{Isothermal} & \text{(I)} & \Delta \mathrm{W} \text{(work done)} =0 \\ \hline \text{(B)} & \text{Adiabatic} & \text{(II)} & \Delta \mathrm{Q} \text{(supplied heat)} =0 \\ \hline \text{(C)} & \text{Isobaric} & \text{(III)} & \begin{array}{l}\Delta \mathrm{U} \text{(change in internal} \\ \text{energy} \neq 0\end{array} \\ \hline \text{(D)} & \text{Isochoric} & \text{(IV)} & \Delta \mathrm{U}=0 \\ \hline\end{array}$
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** (A)-(III), (B)-(II), (C)-(I), (D)-(IV)
- **(B)** (A)-(IV), (B)-(I), (C)-(III), (D)-(II)
- **(C)** (A)-(IV), (B)-(II), (C)-(III), (D)-(I)
- **(D)** (A)-(II), (B)-(IV), (C)-(I), (D)-(III)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) (A)-(IV), (B)-(II), (C)-(III), (D)-(I)**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A) Isothermal $\rightarrow \Delta \mathrm{T}=0 \rightarrow \Delta \mathrm{U}=0$ (IV)
(B) Adiabatic $\rightarrow \Delta \mathrm{Q}=0$ (II)
(C) Isobaric $\rightarrow \Delta \mathrm{P}=0 \rightarrow \Delta \mathrm{U} \neq 0$ (III)
(D) Isochoric $\rightarrow \Delta \mathrm{V}=0 \rightarrow \Delta \mathrm{~W}=0(\mathrm{I})$

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2025 (4 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Match List-I with List-II.
\(\begin{array}{|l|l|l|l|} \hline & \text{List - I} & & \text{List - II} \\ \hline \text { (A) } & \text { Isobaric } & \text { (I) } & \Delta Q=\Delta W \\ \hline \text { (B) } & \text { Isochoric } & \text { (II) } & \Delta Q=\Delta U \\ \hline \text { (C) } & \text { Adiabatic } & \text { (III) } & \Delta Q=\text { zero } \\ \hline \text { (D) } & \text { Isothermal } & \text { (IV) } & \Delta Q=\Delta U+P \Delta V \\ \hline \end{array}\)
$\Delta \mathrm{Q}=$ Heat supplied
$\Delta \mathrm{W}=$ Work done by the system
$\Delta \mathrm{U}=$ Change in internal energy
$\mathrm{P}=$ Pressure of the system
$\Delta \mathrm{V}=$ Change in volume of the system
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** (A)-(IV), (B)-(III), (C)-(II), (D)-(I)
- **(B)** (A)-(IV), (B)-(I), (C)-(III), (D)-(II)
- **(C)** (A)-(IV), (B)-(II), (C)-(III), (D)-(I)
- **(D)** (A)-(II), (B)-(IV), (C)-(III), (D)-(I)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) (A)-(IV), (B)-(II), (C)-(III), (D)-(I)**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A) Isobaric $(\mathrm{P}=\mathrm{C})$
$\Delta \mathrm{Q}=\Delta \mathrm{U}+\mathrm{P} \Delta \mathrm{~V}$
(B) Isochoric $(\mathrm{V}=\mathrm{C})$
$\Delta \mathrm{Q}=\Delta \mathrm{U}$
(C) Adiabatic $(\Delta \mathrm{Q}=0)$
$\Delta \mathrm{Q}=0$
(D) Isothermal $(\Delta \mathrm{U}=0)$
$\Delta \mathrm{Q}=\Delta \mathrm{W}$

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An ideal gas exists in a state with pressure $P_0$ volume $\mathrm{V}_0$.It is isothermally expanded to 4 times of its initial volume $\left(\mathrm{V}_0\right)$, then isobarically compressed to its original volume. Finally the system is heated isochorically to bring it to its initial state. The amount of heat exchanged in this process is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{~V}_0(2 \ln 2-0.75)$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{~V}_0(\ln 2-0.75)$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{~V}_0(\ln 2-0.25)$
- **(D)** $P_0 V_0(2 \ln 2-0.25)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{~V}_0(2 \ln 2-0.75)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/rbb3JpnqJ0LtZIcUiwPULzDiFzpxHYbMbm338lcPV-s.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\begin{aligned} & \omega_1=P_0 v_0 \ln 4 \\ & \omega_2=\frac{P_0}{4}\left(-3 v_0\right)=-\frac{3 P_0 v_0}{4}\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \omega_3=0 \\ & \mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{T}}=\Delta \mathrm{U}_{\text {cyclic }}+\omega\end{aligned}$
$\mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{T}}=\omega \quad\left(\Delta \mathrm{U}_{\text {cyclic }}=0\right)$
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{T}}=\mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{v}_0\left(\ln 4-\frac{3}{4}\right) \\ & =\mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{v}_0(2 \ln 2-0.75)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Pressure of an ideal gas, contained in a closed vessel, is increased by $0.4 \%$ when heated by $1^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. Its initial temperature must be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $2500\mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $250\mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $250^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $250\mathrm{~K}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Isochoric process
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{P} \propto \mathrm{T} \\ & \frac{\Delta \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{T}}{\mathrm{T}}\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{0.4}{100}=\frac{1}{T} \\ & T=250 \mathrm{~K}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q19. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A gas is kept in a container having walls which are thermally non-conducting. Initially the gas has a volume of $800 \mathrm{~cm}^3$ and temperature $27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. The change in temperature when the gas is adiabatically compressed to $200 \mathrm{~cm}^3$ is :
(Take $\gamma=1.5: \gamma$ is the ratio of specific heats at constant pressure and at constant volume)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 327 K
- **(B)** 600 K
- **(C)** 522 K
- **(D)** 300 K

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 300 K**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{V}_1=800 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \quad \mathrm{~V}_2=200 \mathrm{~cm}^3 \\
& \mathrm{~T}_1=300 \mathrm{~K}
\end{aligned}$
for adiabatic
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{TV}^{\gamma-1}=\text { const. } \\
& (300)(800)^{1.5-1}=\mathrm{T}_2(200)^{1.5-1} \\
& \mathrm{~T}_2=300\left[\frac{800}{200}\right]^{0.5}=300 \times\left(2^2\right)^{1 / 2} \\
& \mathrm{~T}_2=600 \mathrm{~K} \\
& \Delta \mathrm{~T}=600-300=300 \mathrm{~K}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Identify the characteristics of an adiabatic process in a monoatomic gas.
(A) Internal energy is constant.
(B) Work done in the process is equal to the charge in internal energy.
(C) The product of temperature and volume is a constant.
(D) The product of pressure and volume is a constant.
(E) The work done to change the temperature from $\mathrm{T}_1$ to $\mathrm{T}_2$ is proportional to $\left(\mathrm{T}_2-\mathrm{T}_1\right)$
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** (A), (C), (D) only
- **(B)** (A), (C), (E) only
- **(C)** (B), (E) only
- **(D)** (B), (D) only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) (B), (E) only**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{Q}=\Delta \mathrm{U}+\mathrm{W}=0 \Rightarrow-\Delta \mathrm{U}=\mathrm{W} \\ & \mathrm{WD}=-\mathrm{nC}_{\mathrm{v}} \Delta \mathrm{T} \Rightarrow|\mathrm{WD}|=\mathrm{nC}_{\mathrm{v}} \Delta \mathrm{T} \propto \mathrm{T}_2-\mathrm{T}_1\end{aligned}$
$\therefore \mathrm{B} \& \mathrm{E}$ [Only possibility]

</details>

---

### Q21. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In an adiabatic process, which of the following statements is true?

**Options:**

- **(A)** The molar heat capacity is infinite
- **(B)** Work done by the gas equals the increase in internal energy
- **(C)** The molar heat capacity is zero
- **(D)** The internal energy of the gas decreases as the temperature increases

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) The molar heat capacity is zero**

**Detailed Solution:**

For adiabatic process, $\mathrm{dQ}=0$
$\therefore$ Molar heat capacity $=0$
$\because \mathrm{dQ}=0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{dU}=-\mathrm{dW}$
Also $\mathrm{dU}=\frac{\mathrm{f}}{2} \mathrm{nRdT}$
$\therefore$ Only option (3) is correct.

</details>

---

### Q22. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/n5gJgOGduhZn9_w8BJr8yRsK5UnNoIr7xi53-AHZ-hI.original.fullsize.png"/>

A poly-atomic molecule ( $C_V=3 R, C_P=4 R$, where $R$ is gas constant) goes from phase space point $\mathrm{A}\left(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{A}}=10^5 \mathrm{~Pa}, \mathrm{~V}_{\mathrm{A}}=4 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~m}^3\right)$ to point $\mathrm{B}\left(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{B}}=5 \times 10^4 \mathrm{~Pa}, \mathrm{~V}_{\mathrm{B}}=6 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~m}^3\right)$ to point $\mathrm{C}\left(\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{C}}=10^4\right.$ $\left.\mathrm{Pa}, \mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{C}}=8 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~m}^3\right)$. A to B is an adiabatic path and $B$ to $C$ is an isothermal path.
The net heat absorbed per unit mole by the system is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $500 \mathrm{R}(\ln 3+\ln 4)$
- **(B)** $450 \mathrm{R}(\ln 4-\ln 3)$
- **(C)** $500 \mathrm{R} \ln 2$
- **(D)** $400 \mathrm{R} \ln 4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $450 \mathrm{R}(\ln 4-\ln 3)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{AB}}=0 \text { adiabatic } \\ & \Delta \mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{BC}}=\Delta \mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{BC}} \\ & =\mathrm{nRT} \ell \mathrm{n}\left(\frac{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{C}}}{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{B}}}\right)=450 \mathrm{R} \ell \mathrm{n}\left(\frac{8 \times 10^{-6}}{6 \times 10^{-6}}\right) \\ & =450 \mathrm{R} \ell \mathrm{n}\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)=450 \mathrm{R}(\ln 4-\ell \mathrm{n} 3) \\ & \therefore \Delta \mathrm{Q}=\Delta \mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{AB}}+\Delta \mathrm{Q}_{\mathrm{BC}} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{Q}=450 \mathrm{R}(\ell \mathrm{n} 4-\ell \mathrm{n} 3)\end{aligned}$

</details>

Given below are two statements. One is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other is labelled as Reason (R).

In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

- **(D)** (A) is false but (R) is true

### Q24. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A container of fixed volume contains a gas at \(27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\). To double the pressure of the gas, the temperature of gas should be raised to ______ \({ }^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **327**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} V & =\text { constant } \\ \frac{P_1}{T_1} & =\frac{P_2}{T_2} \\ P_2 & =2 P_1 \\ T_2 & =2 T_1 \\ & =2 \times 300=600 \mathrm{~K} \\ \therefore T_2 & =327^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\end{aligned}

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The workdone in an adiabatic change in an ideal gas depends upon only :

**Options:**

- **(A)** change in its temperature
- **(B)** change in its volume
- **(C)** change in its pressure
- **(D)** change in its specific heat

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) change in its temperature**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done in adiabatic process $=\frac{n R \Delta T}{1-\gamma}$
So, depends upon change in temperature.

</details>

### Q26. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

An ideal gas initially at $0^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ temperature, is compressed suddenly to one fourth of its volume. If the ratio of specific heat at constant pressure to that at constant volume is $3 / 2$, the change in temperature due to the thermodynamic process is _____ K.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **273**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \gamma=\frac{3}{2} \\ & \mathrm{Tv}^{\gamma-1}=\mathrm{C} \\ & 273 \mathrm{~V}_0^{0.5}=\mathrm{T}\left(\frac{\mathrm{V}_0}{4}\right)^{0.5} \\ & \mathrm{~T}=273 \times 2=546 \\ & \Delta \mathrm{~T}=273\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A real gas within a closed chamber at $27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ undergoes the cyclic process as shown in figure. The gas obeys $P V^3=R T$ equation for the path $A$ to $B$. The net work done in the complete cycle is (assuming $R=8 \mathrm{~J} / \mathrm{mol} \mathrm{K}$ ):
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/MaShLXI7MYXTuG2HJc9_KIJxN1dVYeOQKqX0uLnKwWc.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $20 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(B)** $205 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(C)** $-20 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(D)** $225 \mathrm{~J}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $205 \mathrm{~J}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{AB}}=\int \mathrm{PdV} \quad$ (Assuming $\mathrm{T}$ to be constant)
$\begin{aligned} & =\int \frac{\mathrm{RTdV}}{\mathrm{V}^3} \\ & =\mathrm{RT} \int_2^4 \mathrm{~V}^{-3} \mathrm{dV} \\ & =8 \times 300 \times\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{1}{4^2}-\frac{1}{2^2}\right]\right) \\ & =225 \mathrm{~J}\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned}& \mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{BC}}=\mathrm{P} \int_4^2 \mathrm{dV}=10(2-4)=-20 \mathrm{~J} \\& \mathrm{~W}_{\mathrm{CA}}=0 \\& \therefore \mathrm{W}_{\text {cycle }}=205 \mathrm{~J}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The volume of an ideal gas $(\gamma=1.5)$ is changed adiabatically from 5 litres to 4 litres. The ratio of initial pressure to final pressure is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{16}{25}$
- **(B)** $\frac{4}{5}$
- **(C)** $\frac{8}{5 \sqrt{5}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{2}{\sqrt{5}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{8}{5 \sqrt{5}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For Adiabatic process
$\begin{aligned} & P_i V_i=P_f V_f^\gamma \\ & P_i(5)^{1.5}=P_f(4)^{1.5} \\ & \frac{P_i}{P_f}=\left(\frac{4}{5}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}=\frac{4}{5} \cdot\left(\frac{4}{5}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}} \Rightarrow \frac{8}{5 \sqrt{5}}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q29. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A sample of 1 mole gas at temperature $\mathrm{T}$ is adiabatically expanded to double its volume. If adiabatic constant for the gas is $\gamma=\frac{3}{2}$, then the work done by the gas in the process is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{R}{T}[2-\sqrt{2}]$
- **(B)** $\frac{T}{R}[2+\sqrt{2}]$
- **(C)** RT $[2-\sqrt{2}]$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{RT}[2+\sqrt{2}]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) RT $[2-\sqrt{2}]$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{TV}^{\gamma-1}=\text { constant } \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{T}(\mathrm{V})^{\frac{3}{2}-1}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}(2 \mathrm{~V})^{\frac{3}{2}-1} \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{TV}^{\frac{1}{2}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}(2)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathrm{~V})^{\frac{1}{2}} \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{T}}{\sqrt{2}}\right) \\ & \text { Now, W.D. }=\frac{\mathrm{nR} \Delta \mathrm{T}}{1-\gamma}=\frac{1 \cdot \mathrm{R}\left[\frac{\mathrm{T}}{\sqrt{2}}-\mathrm{T}\right]}{1-\frac{3}{2}} \\ & \Rightarrow \text { W.D. }=2 \mathrm{RT}\left[1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right] \\ & \Rightarrow \text { W.D. }=\mathrm{RT}[2-\sqrt{2}]\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q30. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A diatomic gas $(\gamma=1.4)$ does $100 \mathrm{~J}$ of work in an isobaric expansion. The heat given to the gas is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $250 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(B)** $150 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(C)** $350 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(D)** $490 \mathrm{~J}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $350 \mathrm{~J}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For Isobaric process
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{w}=\mathrm{P} \Delta \mathrm{v}=\mathrm{nR} \Delta \mathrm{T}=100 \mathrm{~J} \\ & \mathrm{Q}=\Delta \mathrm{u}+\mathrm{w} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{Q}=\frac{\mathrm{F}}{2} \mathrm{nR} \Delta \mathrm{T}+\mathrm{nR} \Delta \mathrm{T} \\ & \left(\frac{\mathrm{f}}{2}+1\right) \mathrm{nR} \Delta \mathrm{T} \\ & \left(\frac{5}{2}+1\right) 100=350 \mathrm{~J}\end{aligned}$
SECTION - B

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Two different adiabatic paths for the same gas intersect two isothermal curves as shown in P-V diagram. The relation between the ratio $\frac{V_a}{V_d}$ and the ratio $\frac{V_b}{V_c}$ is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4iaSAzfJTlk_pM-k7g3cPrdPRs6eBjPpBl3mPI4SUog.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{V_a}{V_d} \neq \frac{V_b}{V_c}$
- **(B)** $\frac{V_a}{V_d}=\frac{V_b}{V_c}$
- **(C)** $\frac{V_a}{V_d}=\left(\frac{V_b}{V_c}\right)^{-1}$
- **(D)** $\frac{V_a}{V_d}=\left(\frac{V_b}{V_c}\right)^2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{V_a}{V_d}=\frac{V_b}{V_c}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For adiabatic process
$\mathrm{TV}^{\gamma-1}=$ constant
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{a}}^{\gamma-1}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{d}} \cdot \mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\gamma-1} \\ & \left(\frac{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{a}}}{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{d}}}\right)^{\gamma-1}=\frac{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{d}}}{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{a}}} \\ & \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{b}}^{\gamma-1}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{c}} \cdot \mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\gamma-1} \\ & \left(\frac{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{b}}}{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{c}}}\right)^{\gamma-1}=\frac{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{c}}}{\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}} \\ & \frac{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{a}}}{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{d}}}=\frac{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{b}}}{\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{c}}} \quad\left(\begin{array}{c}\because \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{d}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{c}} \\ \mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{a}}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{b}}\end{array}\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The heat absorbed by a system in going through the given cyclic process is :
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/rk_cil8WOBoaFAiS50kFTB3VWKXhqorLZFbmn_cAGWs.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $19.6 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(B)** $61.6 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(C)** $616 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(D)** $431.2 \mathrm{~J}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $61.6 \mathrm{~J}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{U}=0(\text { Cyclic process }) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{Q}=\mathrm{W}=\text { area of } \mathrm{P}-\mathrm{V} \text { curve. } \\ & =\pi \times\left(140 \times 10^3 \mathrm{~Pa}\right) \times\left(140 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~m}^3\right) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{Q}=61.6 \mathrm{~J}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A sample of gas at temperature $T$ is adiabatically expanded to double its volume. Adiabatic constant for the gas is $\gamma=3 / 2$. The work done by the gas in the process is:
$(\mu=1 \mathrm{~mole})$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $R T[1-2 \sqrt{2}]$
- **(B)** $R T[\sqrt{2}-2]$
- **(C)** $R T[2-\sqrt{2}]$
- **(D)** $R T[2 \sqrt{2}-1]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $R T[2-\sqrt{2}]$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{W}=\frac{\mathrm{nR} \Delta \mathrm{T}}{1-\gamma} \\ & \mathrm{TV}^{\gamma-1}=\text { constan } \mathrm{t}=\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{f}}(2 \mathrm{~V})^{\gamma-1} \\ & \mathrm{~T}_{\mathrm{f}}=\mathrm{T}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{1 / 2}=\frac{\mathrm{T}}{\sqrt{2}} \\ & \mathrm{~W}=\frac{\mathrm{R}\left(\frac{\mathrm{T}}{\sqrt{2}}-\mathrm{T}\right)}{1-\frac{3}{2}}=2 \mathrm{RT} \frac{(\sqrt{2}-1)}{\sqrt{2}} \\ & =\mathrm{RT}(2-\sqrt{2})\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q34. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

P-T diagram of an ideal gas having three different densities $\rho_1, \rho_2, \rho_3$ (in three different cases) is shown in the figure. Which of the following is correct :
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/FY3RknQd1DR5i3YKub5pOiXUSbj4Xn7hbwpUytlXyE8.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\rho_1>\rho_2$
- **(B)** $\rho_2 < \rho_3$
- **(C)** $\rho_1=\rho_2=\rho_3$
- **(D)** $\rho_1 < \rho_2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\rho_1>\rho_2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For ideal gas
$\begin{aligned}
& P V=n R T \\
& P V=\frac{m}{M} R T \\
& P=\left(\frac{M}{V}\right) \frac{R T}{M} \\
& P=\frac{\rho R T}{M}
\end{aligned}$
(Where $\mathrm{m}$ is mass of gas and $\mathrm{M}$ is molecular mass of gas)
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/sB72bMqLldNuXVQOFMuzIj48-a6mJ8Tb91CeUaR8zt0.original.fullsize.png">
for same temperature $\mathrm{P}_1>\mathrm{P}_2>\mathrm{P}_3$
So $\rho_1>\rho_2>\rho_3$

</details>

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>800</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

### Q36. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The pressure and volume of an ideal gas are related as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi></math> (Constant). The work done when the gas is taken from state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> to state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msqrt><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>−</mo><msqrt><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msqrt><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></msqrt><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>x</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant, work done by the gas is given by, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>Δ</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>, therefore we can write</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mo>−</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> </p>

</details>

### Q37. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The given figure represents two isobaric processes for the same mass of an ideal gas, then</p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/05dc0f9f-f20a-49af-8454-c5508d494c0a.png" style="width: 250px; height: 200px;"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>≥</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/05dc0f9f-f20a-49af-8454-c5508d494c0a.png" style="width: 250px; height: 200px;"/></p><p>As we know, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mi>P</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>Therefore, slope <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mi>P</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Or slope <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>P</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mi>Slope</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mfenced><mi>Slope</mi></mfenced><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Choose the correct statement for processes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> &amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> shown in figure.</p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/748c6504-0967-44db-a21e-dd004831a109.png" style="width: 200px; height: 188px;"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>γ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>.
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>P</mi><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>T</mi><mi>γ</mi></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>T</mi><mi>γ</mi></msup><msup><mi>P</mi><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>γ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math> for process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Steeper curve (B) is adiabatic
For adiabatic <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><msup><mi>PV</mi><mi>γ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant</p><p>and curve (A) is isothermal. Therefore,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo></math> constant
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo></math> constant</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q39. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

During an adiabatic process, the pressure of a gas is found to be proportional to the cube of its absolute temperature. The ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub></mfrac></math> for the gas is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>It is given that</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>∝</mo><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>T</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>For an adiabatic process,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>γ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Assuming the gas to be a perfect gas, it can be written from equation (2) that</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mi>P</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mi>γ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><msup><mi>P</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>γ</mi></mrow></msup><msup><mi>T</mi><mi>γ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>T</mi><mfrac><mi>γ</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>γ</mi></mrow></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext></math></p><p>By comparing with equation(1), we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mi>γ</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>γ</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mi>γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>γ</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>γ</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mi>γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q40. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>The initial pressure and volume of an ideal gas are<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>. The final pressure of the gas when the gas is suddenly compressed to volume<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math> will be:</p><p>(Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi></math>= ratio of specific heats at constant pressure and at constant volume.)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></msup></math></p>
- **(B)** <p>  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p>
- **(C)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p>
- **(D)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#947;</mi></mfrac></msup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></msup></math></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>As the gas is suddenly compressed the process is adiabatic.</p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>constant</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn><mi>&#947;</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>4</mn><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q41. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The thermodynamic process, in which internal energy of the system remains constant is

**Options:**

- **(A)** Isochoric
- **(B)** Adiabatic
- **(C)** Isothermal
- **(D)** Isobaric

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Isothermal**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>An isothermal process is a thermodynamic process in which the temperature of a system remains constant. The transfer of heat into or out of the system happens so slowly that thermal equilibrium is maintained.</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.</p><p>According to first law of thermodynamics,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>W</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>W</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q42. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A gas is compressed adiabatically, which one of the following statement is NOT true?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** There is no heat supplied to the system
- **(B)** There is no change in the internal energy
- **(C)** <p>The temperature of the gas increases</p>
- **(D)** The change in the internal energy is equal to the work done on the gas

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) There is no change in the internal energy**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>In adiabatic process,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Using the first law of thermodynamics, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>is&#160;the&#160;work&#160;done&#160;by&#160;the&#160;gas</mtext><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>so</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>will&#160;be&#160;the&#160;work&#160;done&#160;on&#160;the&#160;gas</mtext></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>As the gas is compressed</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q43. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Consider two containers <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> containing monoatomic gases at the same Pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>P</mi></mfenced></math>, Volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>V</mi></mfenced></math> and Temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>T</mi></mfenced></math>. The gas in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is compressed isothermally to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math> of its original volume while the gas in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is compressed adiabatically to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math> of its original volume. The ratio of final pressure of gas in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> to that of gas in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>8</mn><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>In an isothermal process the temperature is constant.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>So, it can be written that </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mfenced><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>P</mi></math></p><p>In an adiabatic process,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math>constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi></math> for monoatomic gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So, it can be written</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mi>P</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>32</mn><mi>P</mi></math></p><p>Thus, the ratio of the pressures is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>32</mn><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>P</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q44. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>For three low density gases <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> pressure versus temperature graphs are plotted while keeping them at constant volume, as shown in the figure</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/50ad71df-1295-4531-9fa8-356adf8a6b98-9896704.png" style="width: 350px; height: 179px;" /></p><p>The temperature corresponding to the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#39;</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></math> is:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>373</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In case of constant volume process, i.e., isochoric process, </p><p>From ideal gas equation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>P</mi><mi>T</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mi>V</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math>constant</p><p>Or we have, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>273</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p>Thus, when temperature goes to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>, all constituent particles get freezed and pressure goes to zero irrespective of the density of gas.</p><p>Hence, the temperature corresponding to the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q45. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A sample of gas at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> is adiabatically expanded to double its volume. The work done by the gas in the process is given, (given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>) :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><mi>R</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>T</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Work done by gas in adiabatic process is:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p> For an adiabatic process, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>T</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Now, work done is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi>T</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mstyle></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q46. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A hypothetical gas expands adiabatically such that its volume changes from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>08</mn></math> litres to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn></math> litres. If the ratio of final pressure of the gas to initial pressure of the gas is<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>16</mn><mn>81</mn></mfrac></math> . Then the ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub></mfrac></math> will be.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi></math> be the ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Then for adiabatic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo></math>Constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mn>81</mn><mn>16</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>27</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup></mfenced><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>27</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"/></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q47. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Heat energy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>735</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> is given to a diatomic gas allowing the gas to expand at constant pressure. Each gas molecule rotates around an internal axis but do not oscillate. The increase in the internal energy of the gas will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>525</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>441</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>572</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>735</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>525</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For isobaric process and diatomic gas we can write, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>P</mi></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>735</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>n</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>735</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>210</mn></math></p><p>Now internal energy will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>210</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>525</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q48. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The correct relation between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>P</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mfrac></math> and temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>T</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>T</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The ratio of molar specific heat capacity at constant pressure to the molar specific heat capacity at constant volume is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>P</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math></p><p>where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi></math> is constant for a given gas.</p><p>Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>P</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q49. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A bicycle tyre is filled with air having pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>270</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The approximate pressure of the air in the tyre when the temperature increases to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>36</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>270</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>262</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>278</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>360</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>278</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>270</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>27</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>273</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>309</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p>Ideal gas equation is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></math>.</p><p>Considering isochoric process, i.e., constant volume so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>Thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>270</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mn>309</mn></mfenced></mrow><mn>300</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>278</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q50. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Match List I with List II :</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 600px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td> </td>			<td><strong>List I</strong></td>			<td> </td>			<td><strong>List II</strong></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A</td>			<td>Isothermal Process</td>			<td>I</td>			<td>Work done by the gas decreases internal energy</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B</td>			<td>Adiabatic Process</td>			<td>II</td>			<td>No change in internal energy</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C</td>			<td>Isochoric Process</td>			<td>III</td>			<td>The heat absorbed goes partly to increase internal energy and partly to do work</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D</td>			<td>Isobaric Process</td>			<td>IV</td>			<td>No work is done on or by the gas</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-II, B-I, C-III, D-IV
- **(B)** A-II, B-I, C-IV, D-III
- **(C)** A-I, B-II, C-IV, D-III
- **(D)** A-I, B-II, C-III, D-IV

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A-II, B-I, C-IV, D-III**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(A) Change in internal energy is expressed as<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>, here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi></math> is change in temperature.</p><p>Since, in an isothermal process temperature remains constant, thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>I</mi><mi>I</mi></math></p><p>(B) In an adiabatic process, heat transfer, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.</p><p>So, from first law of thermodynamics, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>W</mi></math></p><p>Since, work done by gas is positive, thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi></math> is negative</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>I</mi></math></p><p>(C) In an isochoric process, volume remains constant, so work done by or on the gas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>I</mi><mi>V</mi></math></p><p>(D) In an isobaric process, pressure remains constant, so work done<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and change in internal energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Thus, heat absorbed goes partly to increase internal energy and partly do work.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>I</mi><mi>I</mi><mi>I</mi></math></p>

</details>
