### Q51. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In an Isothermal change, the change in pressure and volume of a gas can be represented for three different temperature; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> as:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b6b6cc63-e289-4355-9b8b-946b2604511b-9894240.PNG" style="width: 174px; height: 180px;" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/44a259c5-c222-41bd-8812-11ebf3d2bf0f-9894240_1.PNG" style="width: 175px; height: 180px;" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/96136b78-f06b-4c49-9ca4-9b6efda284cf-9894240_2.PNG" style="width: 175px; height: 180px;" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fbb35a83-fdf7-47f3-82c9-e0d23deae04e-9894240_3.PNG" style="width: 173px; height: 180px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fbb35a83-fdf7-47f3-82c9-e0d23deae04e-9894240_3.PNG" style="width: 173px; height: 180px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In case of isothermal process, pressure vs volume graph is a rectangular hyperbola.</p><p>The graph of the isothermal process shifts away from the origin as the temperature of the process is increased. Thus, the curve away from the origin <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> have the highest temperature whereas the curve closer to the origin <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> have the lowest temperature.</p><p>Here, the dotted line shows the isobaric line that implies <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a127db4c-b436-48da-8688-5b0312b0b339-9894240_4.PNG" style="width: 241px; height: 220px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A thermodynamic system is taken from an original state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi></math> to an intermediate state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> by the linear process shown in the figure. Its volume is then reduced to the original volume from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> by an isobaric process. The total work done by the gas from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> will be</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6e88af83-8b52-4a85-9bef-1f8089e65a6e-image.png" style="width: 240px; height: 195px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>450</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>450</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>900</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1350</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>450</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Total work done by the gas from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math>Area of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>D</mi><mi>E</mi><mi>F</mi></math></p><p>Work done during the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi><mi>E</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mi>DE</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>600</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>300</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1350</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Work done during the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mi>F</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mi>EF</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>900</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mi>DEF</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mi>DE</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mi>EF</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>450</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>The pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and density <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>d</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> of diatomic gas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> changes suddenly to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> respectively during an adiabatic process. The temperature of the gas increases and becomes____times of its initial temperature. </p><p>(given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>32</mn></math>)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>For adiabatic process, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant</p><p>We know, density <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>mass</mi><mi>Volume</mi></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Then, we have <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>d</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math> constant</p><p>Given here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>32</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>.</p><p>The ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>d</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>32</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>128</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>The ratio of temperature using ideal gas equation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>128</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>32</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

At a certain temperature, the degrees of freedom per molecule for gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn></math>. The gas performs <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>150</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of work when it expands under constant pressure. The amount of heat absorbed by the gas will be _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **750**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>For constant pressure process,</p><p>Work done is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>150</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>, here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> is gas constant.</p><p>Now, for above process, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math>.</p><p>So, specific heat at constant pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>P</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>R</mi></math>.</p><p>Amount of heat adsorbed by the gas is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>P</mi></msub><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>150</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>750</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q55. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A monoatomic gas at pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> is suddenly compressed to one eighth of its original volume. The final pressure at constant entropy will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi>P</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn><mi>P</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>64</mn><mi>P</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn><mi>P</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Change in entropy is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>d</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>d</mo><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mi>T</mi></mfrac></math>. Since, the process is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>d</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>d</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. Therefore, constant entropy means the process is adiabatic.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math>constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn><mi>&#947;</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn><mi>&#947;</mi></msubsup></math></p><p>For a monatomic gas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>8</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle></msubsup></mrow><mn>32</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>32</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q56. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math> mole of certain monoatomic ideal gas undergoes a temperature increase of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> at constant pressure. The increase in the internal energy of the gas in this process is
(Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5810</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3486</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>11620</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6972</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3486</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>For the given process pressure is constant therefore, it is an isobaric process.</p><p>For a quasi-static process the change in internal energy of an ideal gas is independent of the nature of the process and is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>[molar heat capacity at constant volume for monatomic gas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>]</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>3486</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A certain amount of gas of volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> temperature and pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> expands isothermally until its volume gets doubled. Later it expands adiabatically until its volume gets redoubled. The final pressure of the gas will be (Use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>536</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>536</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>536</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math></p><p>Using Boyle&#39;s law,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>Since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>.</p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> (isothermal expansion)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math></p><p>Now, during adiabatic expansion, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup></mrow><msup><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>536</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q58. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Starting with the same initial conditions, an ideal gas expands from volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> in three different ways. The work done by the gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> if the process is purely isothermal, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>, if the process is purely adiabatic and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> if the process is purely isobaric. Then, choose the correct option

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As we know, in isobaric process pressure remains constant and magnitude of slope of adiabatic curve will be more than the isothermal curve at the common point, therefore diagram for process will be as given below.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/867db2a6-a4da-4624-aeaa-b5fad7e5a5ff-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 161px;" /></p><p>Also, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo></math> area under <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> curve.</p><p>Hence,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q59. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A sample of an ideal gas is taken through the cyclic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math> as shown in figure. It absorbs, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of heat during the part <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math>, no heat during <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math> and rejects <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of heat during <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math>. A work of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> is done on the gas during the part <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math>. The internal energy of the gas at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1560</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. The work done by the gas during the part <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math> is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a34d352d-82ef-450a-a47a-09a0cf5845cc-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 150px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p> In the part <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>B</mi></math>, the volume remains constant. Thus, the work done by the gas is zero. It is given that the heat absorbed by the gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. From first law of thermodynamics, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>The increase in internal energy from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. The internal energy is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1560</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>, then</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1560</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>In the part <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>C</mi></math>, the work done by the gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> and no heat is given to the system. Again using <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>50</mn></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1600</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1650</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p> The change in internal energy from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>A</mi></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1560</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1650</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>The heat given to the system is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Using <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math>, we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>90</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q60. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The total internal energy of two mole monoatomic ideal gas at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> will be _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. (Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>31</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **7479**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The internal energy of an ideal gas is a state function and is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mi>T</mi></math>.</p><p>For monatomic gas, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi></math>.</p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>31</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>7479</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q61. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p><strong>Statement - I :</strong> When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi></math> amount of an ideal gas undergoes adiabatic change from state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> to state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>, then work done is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>R</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>P</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo></math> universal gas constant.</p><p><strong>Statement - II :</strong> In the above case, when work is done on the gas, the temperature of the gas would rise.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both statement-I and statement-II are true.
- **(B)** Both statement-I and statement-II are false.
- **(C)** Statement-I is true but statement-II is false.
- **(D)** Statement-I is false but statement-II is true.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both statement-I and statement-II are true.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The work done in an adiabatic process is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>adiabatic</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8594;</mo></math> statement I is true.</p><p>For adiabatic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. From the first law of thermodynamics,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>W</mi></math></p><p>If work is done on the gas, i.e. work is negative</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi></math> is positive.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> The temperature will increase. Hence, statement II is also true.</p>

</details>

---

### Q62. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A diatomic gas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> does <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of work when it is expanded isobarically. The heat given to the gas in the process is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1400**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For isobaric process: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>400</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>Also: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math> </p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math>  (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>, for diatomic gas)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>400</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q63. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The width of one of the two slits in a Young&#39;s double slit experiment is three times the other slit. If the amplitude of the light coming from a slit is proportional to the slit-width, the ratio of minimum to maximum intensity in the interference pattern is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given amplitude $\propto$ slit width
Also intensity $\propto(\text { Amplitude })^2 \propto(\text { Slit width })^2$
$\frac{I_1}{I_2}=\left(\frac{3}{1}\right)^2=9 \Rightarrow I_1=9 I_2$
$\frac{I_{\min }}{I_{\max }}=\left(\frac{\sqrt{I_1}-\sqrt{I_2}}{\sqrt{I_1}+\sqrt{I_2}}\right)^2=\left(\frac{3-1}{3+1}\right)^2=\frac{1}{4}=\frac{x}{4}$
$\Rightarrow x=1$

</details>

---

### Q64. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A sample of gas with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> is taken through an adiabatic process in which the volume is compressed from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1200</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msup></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>.</mo></math> If the initial pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kPa</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The absolute value of the workdone by the gas in the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>________</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **480**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1200</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KPa</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>?</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>1200</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>300</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>4</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1600</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>f</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1200</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>240</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>480</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>240</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>480</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>480</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q65. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

One mole of an ideal gas is taken through an adiabatic process where the temperature rises from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>37</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. If the ideal gas is composed of polyatomic molecule that has 4 vibrational modes, which of the following is true? <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>314</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** work done by the gas is close to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>332</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** work done on the gas is close to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>582</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** work done by the gas is close to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>582</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** work done on the gas is close to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>332</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) work done on the gas is close to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>582</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Since, each vibrational mode, corresponds to two degrees of freedom, hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>=3&#160;(</mo><mi>trans</mi><mo>.)&#160;+3(&#160;</mo><mi>rot</mi><mo>.&#160;)+8(&#160;</mo><mi>vib</mi><mo>.&#160;)=14</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>14</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>nR</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>582</mn></math></p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. work is done on the gas.</p>

</details>

### Q66. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>In the reported figure, there is a cyclic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>D</mi><mi>A</mi></math> on a sample of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mol of a diatomic gas. The temperature of the gas during the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>D</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> respectively.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d41690be-d8fb-41c8-a0b0-7f3cbdb21800-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 239px;" /></p><p>Choose the correct option out of the following for work done if processes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi><mi>A</mi></math> are adiabatic.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>D</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>D</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>D</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>D</mi></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>D</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Work done in adiabatic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>D</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>D</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q67. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A monoatomic ideal gas, initially at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is enclosed in a cylinder fitted with a frictionless piston. The gas is allowed to expand adiabatically to a temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> by releasing the piston suddenly. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are the lengths of the gas column, before and after the expansion respectively, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math> will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>r</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><msup><mstyle mathvariant="italic"><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>&#8467;</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mstyle><mrow><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>&#8467;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#8467;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>In the reported figure, heat energy absorbed by a system in going through a cyclic process is ______<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/168efa09-01b4-42ba-9e02-14273c3af455-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 239px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **100**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/289ed67c-65f6-46e3-a82a-5ac11f730f78-image.png" style="width: 337px; height: 275px;" /></p><p>For complete cyclic process
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math>Area
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Answer <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For an adiabatic expansion of an ideal gas, the fractional change in its pressure is equal to (where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi></math> is the ratio of specific heats):

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mfrac><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#947;</mi></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo mathvariant="italic">&#8658;</mo><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant</p><p>Differentiating, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mi>P</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>P</mi></mrow><mi>P</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram of a diatomic ideal gas system going under cyclic process as shown in figure. The work done during an adiabatic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mi>D</mi></math> is (use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math> :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7a059d70-f1b4-4183-b5a3-c6bc8f12851f-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 254px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Adiabatic process is from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>D</mi></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>D</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>200</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q72. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Which one is the correct option for the two different thermodynamic processes ?</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ce3f05ca-128c-41ae-8624-06aaa4766c4e-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 486px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (c) and (a)
- **(B)** (c) and (d)
- **(C)** (a) only
- **(D)** (b) and (c)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (c) and (d)**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Option (a) is wrong; since in adiabatic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo></math> constant.</p><p>Option (b) is wrong, since in the isothermal process
T= constant</p><p>Option (c) &amp; (d) matches isotherms &amp; adiabatic formula :</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">&#947;</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">-</mo><mn mathvariant="italic">1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>constant</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>T</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><msup><mi>p</mi><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>constant</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q73. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Thermodynamic process is shown below on a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram for one mole of an ideal gas. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, then the ratio of temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></math> is :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c12f95f7-47c6-427c-8bcf-2fb390575cba-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 196px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c12f95f7-47c6-427c-8bcf-2fb390575cba-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 196px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

### Q74. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> mole of a perfect gas undergoes a cyclic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math> (see figure) consisting of the following processes.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>:</mo></math> Isothermal expansion at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> so that the volume is doubled from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and pressure changes from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo></math> Isobaric compression at pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> to initial volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>:</mo></math> Isochoric change leading to change of pressure from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p>Total work done in the complete cycle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math> is:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/89ffb312-e807-4ae0-b59d-be6dac55c474-image.png" style="width: 270px; height: 222px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>isothermal</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo mathvariant="italic">nRT</mo><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced></math>\</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>isobaric</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>isochoric</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/41577bdb-4fa0-45dc-84b7-ef3d995f5f8f-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 200px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mfrac><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>net</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>net</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q75. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Match List <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> with List <strong><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi><mo>.</mo></math></strong></p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;">List<strong> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math></strong></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;">List<strong> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math></strong></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td> (a)</td>			<td> Isothermal</td>			<td> (i)</td>			<td> Pressure constant</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td> (b)</td>			<td> Isochoric</td>			<td> (ii)</td>			<td> Temperature constant</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td> (c)</td>			<td> Adiabatic</td>			<td> (iii)</td>			<td> Volume constant</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td> (d)</td>			<td> Isobaric</td>			<td> (iv)</td>			<td> Heat content is constant</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iv</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iv</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iv</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iv</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iv</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></math> Isothermal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math>Temperature constant
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>(b) Isochoric <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Volume constant
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>iii</mi></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo></math> Adiabatic <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math>Heat content is constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>iv</mi></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo></math> Isobaric<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math>Pressure constant</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q76. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Initially a gas of diatomic molecules is contained in a cylinder of volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> at a pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. Assuming that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of the molecules get dissociated causing a change in number of moles. The pressure of the resulting gas at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo></math> when contained in a volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. The ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is -

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>250</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>no</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2000</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>RT</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>RT</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>250</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>2000</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>
equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>/</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>250</mn><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2000</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>
