### Q77. JEE Main 2020 (05 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In an adiabatic process, the density of a diatomic gas becomes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> times its initial value. The final pressure of the gas is found to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> times the initial pressure. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>326</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>128</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>32</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>128</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p>Final density of diatomic gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>32</mn><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></math></p><p>Final pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></math></p><p>In adiabatic process, equation of state is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> is the volume, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is the pressure and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math> is the ratio of specific heat of diatomic gas</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>&#961;</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math>, As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>&#961;</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>and mass is constant, then the pressure is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><msub><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>32</mn><msub><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><msub><mi>&#961;</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mfrac></mfenced><msup><mrow></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>32</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>128</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The change in the magnitude of the volume of an ideal gas when a small additional pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> is applied at a constant temperature, is the same as the change when the temperature is reduced by a small quantity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> at constant pressure. The initial temperature and pressure of the gas were <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math> respectively. If  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>&#916;T</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>|</mo><mi>&#916;P</mi><mo>|</mo></math> then value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>atm</mi><mo>)</mo></math>  is __________

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **150**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>PV</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>nRT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P&#916;V</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>V&#916;P</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#916;P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>  In second case</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P&#916;V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>nR&#916;T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>nR&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>equating <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>nR&#916;T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#916;P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;P</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi>nR</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi>nR</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Putting the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>150</mn></math> </p>

</details>

### Q79. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Match the thermodynamics processes taking place in a system with the correct conditions. In the table : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>is the heat supplied, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>W</mi></math> is the work done and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi></math> is change in internal energy of the system.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 350px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td> </td>			<td>Process</td>			<td> </td>			<td>Condition</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(I)</td>			<td>Adiabatic</td>			<td>(A)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(II)</td>			<td>Isothermal</td>			<td>(B)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(III)</td>			<td>Isochoric</td>			<td>(C)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(IV)</td>			<td>			<p>Isobaric</p>			</td>			<td>(D)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (I) - (A), (II) - (B), (III) - (D), (IV) - (D)
- **(B)** (I) - (B), (II) - (A), (III) - (D), (IV) - (C)
- **(C)** (I) - (A), (II) - (A), (III) - (B), (IV) - (C)
- **(D)** (I) - (B), (II) - (D), (III) - (A), (IV) - (C)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) (I) - (B), (II) - (D), (III) - (A), (IV) - (C)**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In Adiabatic <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>e</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>,</mo></math> Exchange of heat does not take place.</p><p>In Isothermal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>e</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>,</mo></math> Temperature does not change therefore internal energy also remains constant.</p><p>In Isochoric <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>e</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>,</mo></math> Volume remains constant therefore work done is zero.</p><p>In Isobaric <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>V</mi></math>, Volume changes so work done is not equal to zero.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>V</mi></math>, Volume changes therefore internal energy changes.
 <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>, Temperature changes therefore heat also changes.</p><p> </p><p> </p>

</details>

---

### Q80. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A balloon filled with helium <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>32</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>and</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mn>1</mn><mo>.7</mo><mi>atm</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> bursts. Immediately after wards the expansion of helium can be considered as :

**Options:**

- **(A)** irreversible isothermal
- **(B)** irreversible adiabatic
- **(C)** reversible adiabatic
- **(D)** reversible isothermal

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) irreversible adiabatic**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>A thermodynamic process that happens without transmitting heat or mass between the thermodynamic system and its surroundings is known as an adiabatic process.</p><p>An irreversible process is one in which the system and its surroundings are unable to revert to their original states.</p><p>Bursting of a balloon is an irreversible adiabatic process, because energy can not be restored in this process.</p>

</details>

---

### Q81. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Starting at temperature <math><mn>300</mn><mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>,</mo></math> one mole of an ideal diatomic gas <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is first compressed adiabatically from volume <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> to <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math> It is then allowed to expand isobarically to volume <math><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> . If all the processes are the quasi-static then the final temperature of the gas (in <math><msup><mrow><mi></mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mi>K</mi></math> ) is (to the nearest integer) ___________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1819**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>P</mi><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math>
<math><mi>T</mi><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></math>
<math><mn>300</mn><mi></mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>1.4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mi></mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Now for BC process
<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1818.859</mn></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1819</mn><mi></mi><mi>K</mi></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fefec39d-c84f-42af-b368-089e652d4f11.jpeg" />

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following is an equivalent cyclic process corresponding to the thermodynamic cyclic given in the figure? Where, <math><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn></math> is adiabatic. (Graphs are schematic and are not to scale)

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f07ebaab-f61b-4661-ac7e-a75406af8a0f.jpeg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9495245b-81dd-4980-b243-c7486d5295da.jpeg">
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/41f2a9db-eeb6-4436-b3f7-ffca5d08b75c.jpeg">
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ade121ba-cac8-441c-8ddd-40ce2f0fc99a.jpeg">
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/96a3b841-e141-44c4-8b42-d54b35a9789d.jpeg">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ade121ba-cac8-441c-8ddd-40ce2f0fc99a.jpeg">**

**Detailed Solution:**

For process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, Volume is constant,
<math><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>P</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>;</mo></math> as P increases T increases.
For process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, the process is adiabatic process,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math> Constant
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> Constant.
For process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>3</mn></math>, pressure is constant,
<math><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>T</mi></math>.

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A thermodynamic cycle <math><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi><mi>z</mi><mi>x</mi></math> is shown on a <math><mi>V</mi></math> - <math><mi>T</mi></math> diagram.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d1b1b24f-3b2d-4c64-8e65-2b661e144542-image.png" style="width: 197px; height: 191px;" />
The <math><mi>P</mi></math> - <math><mi>V</mi></math> diagram that best describes this cycle is: (Diagrams are schematic and not to scale)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/19266f58-ac95-46dd-8956-d70769e6ee28-image.png" style="width: 195px; height: 182px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ffed9623-7d9a-45f9-9457-d7058c5fa050-image.png" style="width: 208px; height: 194px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2392293a-f486-42da-b90d-4437a074acdd-image.png" style="width: 204px; height: 186px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6b4ad85f-8eb7-4b7f-992e-c8a92d6c6713-image.png" style="width: 225px; height: 210px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/19266f58-ac95-46dd-8956-d70769e6ee28-image.png" style="width: 195px; height: 182px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>According to the ideal equation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>In the given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8208;</mo><mi>T</mi></math> diagram,</p><p>In process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>y</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>. So, the process is isobaric process.</p><p>In process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>z</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo></math> constant. So, the process is an isochoric process.</p><p>In process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>x</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo></math> constant. So, the process is an isothermal process.</p><p>Analyzing using the ideal gas equation in each process, the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8208;</mo><mi>V</mi></math> graph can be drawn as shown below,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/19266f58-ac95-46dd-8956-d70769e6ee28-image.png" style="width: 195px; height: 182px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Under an adiabatic process, the volume of an ideal gas gets doubled. Consequently, the mean collision time between the gas molecule changes from <math><msub><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> to <math><msub><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> . If <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math> for this gas then a good estimate for <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math> is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Relaxation time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math>
And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q85. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A litre of dry air at STP expands adiabatically to a volume of <math><mn>3</mn></math> litres. If <math><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.40</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi></math> the work done by air is: <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1.4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4.6555</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> [Take air to be an ideal gas]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>60.7</mn><mi></mi><mi>J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>90.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>100.8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>48</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>90.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi></mi><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>273</mn><mi>K</mi></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></msubsup></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>1.4</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Now work done <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math><math><mn>90.5 </mn><mi></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q86. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

One mole of an ideal gas passes through a process where pressure and volume obey the relation <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> . Here <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msub></math> are constants. Calculate the change in the temperature of the gas if its volume changes from <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> to <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msub></math> .

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For ideal gas, <math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>  
Therefore, <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>  
<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>  
Therefore, the change in temperature is: 
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></msub></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A cylinder with fixed capacity of <math><mn>67.2</mn></math> litre contains helium gas at STP. The amount of heat needed to raise the temperature of the gas by <math><mn>20</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is:
[Given that <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8.31</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mi>&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>]</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>748</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>700</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>350</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>374</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>748</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The amount of heat supplied is
<math><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>67.2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>22.4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>20</mn></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8.314</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>748</mn><mtext>&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The given diagram shows four processes i.e., isochoric, isobaric, isothermal and adiabatic. The correct assignment of the processes, in the same order is given by: 
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/26f3d2ea-906c-416d-aa6b-2f9e254a6949-image4.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>→</mo></math> Isobaric process 
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>→</mo></math> Isothermal process 
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>→</mo></math> Adiabatic process 
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>→</mo></math> Isochoric process

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For the given cyclic process <math><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> as shown for a gas, the work done is:

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ltovredjtcxncsznybroozsgnsindnggwvprafyydwzlnsashv.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The work done in a cyclic process is equal to the area of closed P-V curve cycle.</p><p>Work done, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo></math> Area of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>In the given P-V graph, the process is clockwise. So, the work done will be positive which means that the system is doing the work.</p>

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a process, temperature and volume of one mole of an ideal monoatomic gas are varied according to the relation $\mathrm{VT}=\mathrm{K},$ where $\mathrm{K}$ is a constant. In this process the temperature of the gas is increased by $\Delta \mathrm{T}$. The amount of heat absorbed by gas is (R is gas constant):

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{R} \Delta \mathrm{T}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{KR} \Delta \mathrm{T}$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{2} \mathrm{R} \Delta \mathrm{T}$
- **(D)** $\frac{2 \mathrm{~K}}{3} \Delta \mathrm{T}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{R} \Delta \mathrm{T}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to question $\mathrm{VT}=\mathrm{K}$

we also know that $P V=n R T$

$\Rightarrow \mathrm{T}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{PV}}{\mathrm{nR}}\right)$

$\Rightarrow \mathrm{V}\left(\frac{\mathrm{PV}}{\mathrm{nR}}\right)=\mathrm{k} \Rightarrow \mathrm{PV}^{2}=\mathrm{K}$

$\because \mathrm{C}=\frac{\mathrm{R}}{1-\mathrm{x}}+\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{v}}$ (For polytropic process)

$\mathrm{C}=\frac{\mathrm{R}}{1-2}+\frac{3 \mathrm{R}}{2}=\frac{\mathrm{R}}{2}$

$\therefore \Delta \mathrm{Q}=\mathrm{nC} \Delta \mathrm{T}$

$=\frac{R}{2} \times \Delta \mathrm{T}$ [here, $\left.\mathrm{n}=1 \mathrm{~mole}\right]$

</details>

---

### Q91. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A rigid diatomic ideal gas undergoes an adiabatic process at room temperature. The relation between temperature and volume for this process is $\mathrm{TV}^{\mathrm{x}}=$ constant, then $\mathrm{x}$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{5}$
- **(B)** $\frac{2}{5}$
- **(C)** $\frac{2}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{5}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{2}{5}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of adiabatic change is

$$

\mathrm{TV}^{\gamma-1}=\text { constant }

$$

Put $\gamma=\frac{7}{5}$, we get: $\gamma-1=\frac{7}{5}-1$

$\therefore \mathrm{x}=\frac{2}{5}$

</details>

---

### Q92. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Half mole of an ideal monoatomic gas is heated at a constant pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>atm</mi></math> from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. Work done by the gas is<math><mo>(</mo></math>Gas constant,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8.21</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>73</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>581</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>291</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>146</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>291</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Recall the formula of work done in terms of change in volume and pressure, in the case of isobaric process, where pressure is constant, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>P</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, now take the idea of ideal gas equation, 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math><math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8.31</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>70</mn></mrow></mfenced></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>291</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. </p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A gas can be taken from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> via two different processes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ACB</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ADB</mi></math>.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c66bfb8f-12d1-4f88-a003-8d04f817e5fd-D561.png" style="width: 224px; height: 189px;" />

When path <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ACB</mi></math> is used <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of heat flows into the system and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of work is done by the system. If the path <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ADB</mi></math> is used then work done by the system is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>, the heat flows into the system in the path <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ADB</mi></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>80</mn><mi> </mi><mi>J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For path <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ACB</mi></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#916;W</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;U</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>30</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>&#916;U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

For path <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ADB</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#916;W</mi></math>

As initial and find points are the same, change in internal energy is same in both the cases

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

One mole of an ideal monatomic gas is taken along the path <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math> as shown in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi></math> diagram. The maximum temperature attained by the gas along the path <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math> is given by:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d620a2d2-8ad1-431a-8b40-e76c4a11637d-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 177px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>25</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>25</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>25</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>25</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></math>, the temperature <math><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>16</mn></mfrac><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow></mfenced></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>.

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal monatomic gas is compressed isothermally in a rigid vessel to double its pressure at room temperature, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>27</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> . The magnitude of work done on the gas will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mi>R</mi><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mi>ln</mi><mn>6</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mi>R</mi><mi>ln</mi><mn>7</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mi>R</mi><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done on gas<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mi>ln</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mi>R</mi><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>.

</details>

---

### Q96. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 1 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal monoatomic gas is compressed isothermally in a rigid vessel to double its pressure at room temperature, $27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. The work done on the gas will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $300 R \ln 6$
- **(B)** $300 \mathrm{R}$
- **(C)** $300 \mathrm{R} \ln 7$
- **(D)** $300 \mathrm{R} \ln 2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $300 \mathrm{R} \ln 2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done on gas $=\mathrm{nRT} \ell n\left(\frac{p_f}{p_1}\right)$
$$
=\mathrm{R}(300) \ell \mathrm{n}(2)\left(\because \frac{P_f}{p_i}=2 \text { given }\right)
$$

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2018 (08 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Two moles of an ideal monoatomic gas occupies a volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> at <math><msup><mrow><mn>27</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mi>C</mi></math> . The gas expands adiabatically to a volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi></math>. Calculate <math><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo></math> the final temperature of the gas and <math><mo>(</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo></math> change in its internal energy.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>195</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>2.7</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>189</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>2.7</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>195</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2.7</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>189</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2.7</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>189</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2.7</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>T</mi><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>t</mi></math>
<math><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>300</mn><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mi>V</mi></mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>189</mn><mi>K</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8.314</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow></mfenced></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2.7</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi></math>

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2017 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram given for an ideal gas
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/877aca61-5a5c-4884-bc37-a1baa8353ab3-image.png" style="width: 240px; height: 202px;" />
 Out of the following which one correctly represents the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>P</mi></math> diagram?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/26bddddf-17e5-4492-8212-16b826ff1a4f-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 184px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f8fab2e4-571a-4e95-878f-f8a8ed2534bb-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 187px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/99aa1008-df92-4904-b6de-660355df0844-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 183px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8c993e02-4aae-4d84-a7bb-eb04c14a90cd-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 186px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f8fab2e4-571a-4e95-878f-f8a8ed2534bb-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 187px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo></math>constant

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>P</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>

### Q99. JEE Main 2017 (08 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An engine operates by taking <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>moles of an ideal gas through the cycle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>D</mi><mi>A</mi></math> shown in figure. The thermal efficiency of the engine is:

(Take <math><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1.5</mn><mi>R</mi></math>, where<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> is gas constant)

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c6bb157e-535e-4156-b6e9-60379b79931d-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 204px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>32</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done in cycle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math>area under closed curve

<math><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

Heat given <math><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></msub><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>13</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

Thermal efficiency, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#951;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>W</mi><msub><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0.15</mn></math>

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> water is heated from <math><msup><mrow><mn>40</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;o</mi></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> to <math><msup><mrow><mn>60</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;o</mi></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> . Ignoring the slight expansion of water, the change in its internal energy is close to (Given specific heat of water <math><mo>=</mo><mn>4184</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>):

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>167.4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>8.4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4.2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>16.7</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>16.7</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Assuming isochoric process

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4184</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>16.7</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The ratio of work done by an ideal monoatomic gas to the heat supplied to it in an isobaric process is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Work done by an ideal daitomic gas is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>w</mi><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>f</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>g</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>w</mi><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>f</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>g</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>And heat supplies is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>Q</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mi>p</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p>Hence the ratio is given by<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></mrow><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

                                           <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></mrow><msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>
 </p>

</details>

### Q102. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> moles of an ideal gas undergoes a process <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> as shown in the figure. The maximum temperature of the gas during the process will be:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1a78d642-10cf-4ba7-82fe-76b29a02abeb-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 208px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> will be maximum where product of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi></math> is max equation of line

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mi>V</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

Multiplying both sides by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>V</mi></math>

For maxima,           <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mfrac> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub> <mi>P</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi>V</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub> <mi>P</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#8658;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><msub> <mi>V</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow> <mrow> <mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>9</mn><msub> <mi>P</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>4 n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<strong>Alternate method :</strong>

Since initial and final temperature are equal.

Hence maximum temperature is at middle of line.

<math> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mrow> <mi>max</mi></mrow> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>
