### Q103. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal gas goes through a reversible cycle <math><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>d</mi></math> has the V - T diagram shown below. Process <math><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>c</mi></math> are adiabatic.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/feb64732-39cd-4e8b-b354-6accffa0ae44-image.png" style="width: 257px; height: 183px;" />

The corresponding P - V diagram for the process is (all figures are schematic and not drawn to scale) :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a1963d6d-1d3f-4c22-9fb1-419878818f13-image.png" style="width: 186px; height: 187px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/652e2071-53ac-4af2-a942-e391a4d9a005-image.png" style="width: 182px; height: 186px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4620bc1c-09cd-4f56-87b1-d8d1b666fb8f-image.png" style="width: 177px; height: 183px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/55331c29-a7c7-4aea-b98a-7416b4e55e10-image.png" style="width: 184px; height: 189px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a1963d6d-1d3f-4c22-9fb1-419878818f13-image.png" style="width: 186px; height: 187px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

Is an adiabatic process

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>V</mi><msup><mi>T</mi><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> as T increase V decreases at non-uniform rate

In process <math><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>b</mi></math> P = constant as <math><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>

In process <math><mi>c</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>d</mi></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant s <math><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>T</mi></math>

But since slope of V - T graph <math><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

since slope of ab &lt; slope of cd

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></msub></math>

Also in adiabatic process <math><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>a</mi></math> as T is increasing V in decreasing

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> P is increasing, so P - V diagram is as below


<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a1963d6d-1d3f-4c22-9fb1-419878818f13-image.png" style="width: 186px; height: 187px;" />

</details>

---

### Q104. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Consider a spherical shell of radius R at temperature T. The black body radiation inside it can be considered as an ideal gas of photons with internal energy per unit volume <math><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math> and pressure <math><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> . If the shell now undergoes an adiabatic expansion the relation between T and R is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>T</mi><mo>&prop;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>T</mi><mo>∝</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>T</mi><mo>∝</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>T</mi><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi>T</mi><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mo>∵</mo><mi> </mi></math> in an adiabatic process.

<math><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

So by first law of thermodynamics

<math><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></math> ....(i)

Given that <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>∝</mo><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>V</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>d</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>V</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi>V</mi><mi> </mi><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

Also, <math><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>V</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>K</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi></math>

Putting these values in equation (i)

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>K</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi>V</mi><mi> </mi><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>T</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>V</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>V</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ln</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ln</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>T</mi><mi> </mi><mo>⇒</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ln</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ln</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>V</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>π</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext></math>

<math><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q105. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Consider an ideal gas confined in an isolated closed chamber. As the gas undergoes an adiabatic expansion, the average time of collision between molecules increases as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></msup></math> , where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> is the volume of the gas. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math> is:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&gamma;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&gamma;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For an adiabatic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant.
We know that average time of collision between molecules</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#964;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>&#960;</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo></math> number of molecules per unit volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi>rms</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo></math> rms velocity of molecules</p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>&#8733;</mo><msqrt><mi>T</mi></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#964;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mi>V</mi><msqrt><mi>T</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Thus, we can write</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mi>T</mi><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>.</p><p>where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are constants.</p><p>For adiabatic process, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant. Thus, we can write</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#964;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>V</mi><msup><mi>T</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></msup><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>V</mi><msup><mfenced><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></msup></mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math></p><p>or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#964;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi>V</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q106. JEE Main 2014 (19 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A monoatomic gas is compressed from a volume of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math><span style="font-size: 10.8333px;"> </span>to a volume of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> at a constant pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>. Then it is heated at constant volume by supplying <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>150</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of energy. As a result, the internal energy of the gas

**Options:**

- **(A)** Decreases by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** Increases by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** Decreases by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Increases by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Isobaric process with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">W</mtext><mo>+</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">P</mtext><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mo>+</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">P</mtext><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext></math>

      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>J</mtext></math>

      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn><mtext>&#160;J</mtext></math>

Constant volume with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>150</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">Q</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>J</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

              <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>J</mo> </mrow> </math>

Hence, internal energy increases by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q108. JEE Main 2014 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

During an adiabatic compression, $830 \mathrm{~J}$ of work is done on 2 moles of a diatomic ideal gas to reduce its volume by $50 \%$. The change in its temperature is nearly: $\left(\mathrm{R}=8.3 \mathrm{JK}^{-1} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $40 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(B)** $33 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $20 \mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $14 \mathrm{~K}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $20 \mathrm{~K}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given : work done, $W=830 \mathrm{~J}$
No. of moles of gas, $\mu=2$
For diatomic gas $\gamma=1.4$
Work done during an adiabatic change
$$
\begin{aligned}
&W=\frac{\mu R\left(T_1-T_2\right)}{\gamma-1} \\
&\Rightarrow 830=\frac{2 \times 8.3(\Delta T)}{1.4-1}=\frac{2 \times 8.3(\Delta T)}{0.4} \\
&\Rightarrow \Delta T=\frac{830 \times 0.4}{2 \times 8.3}=20 \mathrm{~K}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q109. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The equation of state for a gas is given by <math> <mrow> <mtext>PV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mi>&#945;</mi> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow> </math>, where n is the number of moles and <math> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi></mrow> </math>  is a positive constant. The initial temperature and pressure of one mole of the gas contained in a cylinder are T<sub>0</sub> and P<sub>0</sub> respectively. The work done by the gas when its temperature doubles isobarically will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mo>T</mo> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>ln</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mo>2</mo> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mo>T</mo> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <mo>R</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mo>T</mo> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <mo>R</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mo>T</mo> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <mo>R</mo> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mo>T</mo> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <mo>R</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>0</mtext> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mtext>PV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mtext>dV</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dT</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>dV</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dT</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>dV</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dT</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&there4;   </mo> <mtext>dV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>RdT</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&there4;   </mo> <mtext>W</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&int;</mo> <msub> <mo>P</mo> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mtext>dV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <munderover> <mo>&int;</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </munderover> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>dT</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&alpha;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q110. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>One mole of diatomic ideal gas undergoes a cyclic process ABC as shown in figure. The process BC is adiabatic. The temperatures at A, B and C are 400 K, 800 K and 600 K respectively. Choose the correct statement :

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Q_12_Question_1.jpg" style="width: 200px; height: 205px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** The change in internal energy in whole cyclic process is 250 R
- **(B)** The change in internal energy in the process CA is 700 R.
- **(C)** The change in internal energy in the process AB is  - 350 R
- **(D)** The change in internal energy in the process BC is  - 500 R

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) The change in internal energy in the process BC is  - 500 R**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0025732_Ans_.jpg" style="width: 215px; height: 190px;" />


<math> <mrow> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>CA</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>nc</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mtext>A</mtext> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mtext>C</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>AB</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>nc</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mtext>B</mtext> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mtext>A</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mo>+</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>BC</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>nc</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mtext>C</mtext> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mtext>B</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q111. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A sample of gas expands from $V_1$ to $V_2$. In which of the following, the work done will be greatest?
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/lqXD0vKUfjFklRy0miNMuJZwFFKkuYzMGjnjf3FISY8.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** Same in all processes
- **(B)** Isobaric process
- **(C)** Isothermal process
- **(D)** Adiabatic process

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Isobaric process**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done $=$ Area bounded by PV graph and volume axis
Among the three process, bounded area by PV graph and volume axis is greatest for isobaric process, hence work done is greatest for isobaric process.

</details>

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A certain amount of gas is taken through a cyclic process (A B C D A) that has two isobars, one isochore and one isothermal. The cycle can be represented on a $\mathrm{P}-\mathrm{V}$ indicator diagram as :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/lmr0HKzI7kUVtnwq77y7McRoVsFX8hFgiEgIPFArGsk.original.fullsize.png">
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/OxofYR0EWeoKcNXyRMUdZR0jlyE5xK3BAxdLy4_83Lw.original.fullsize.png">
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/MJT97Gb1GR_l0XJpYqWomQEEwaB5-bsDjD6-FTkCZrY.original.fullsize.png">
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/qvUsIHjYuYvvw398VfJe2BmKUHt-xp8ue_QpNccFDP4.original.fullsize.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/MJT97Gb1GR_l0XJpYqWomQEEwaB5-bsDjD6-FTkCZrY.original.fullsize.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

P-V indicator diagram for isobaric

### Q113. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal gas at atmospheric pressure is adiabatically compressed so that its density becomes 32 times of its initial value. If the final pressure of gas is 128 atmospheres, the value of ' $\gamma$ 'of the gas is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.5$
- **(B)** $1.4$
- **(C)** $1.3$
- **(D)** $1.6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Volume of the gas $\mathrm{v}=\frac{\mathrm{m}}{\mathrm{d}}$ and using $\mathrm{PV}^\gamma=$ constant
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{\mathrm{P}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{P}}=\frac{\mathrm{V}}{\mathrm{V}^{\prime}}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{d}}\right)^\gamma \\
& \text { or } 128=(32)^\gamma \\
& \therefore \gamma=\frac{7}{5}=1.4
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q114. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Figure shows the variation in temperature $(\Delta \mathrm{T})$ with the amount of heat supplied $\mathrm{(Q)}$ in an isobaric process corresponding to a monoatomic $\mathrm{(M)}$, diatomic $\mathrm{(D)}$ and a polyatomic $\mathrm{(P)}$ gas. The initial state of all the gases are the same and the scales for the two axes coincide. Ignoring vibrational degrees of freedom, the lines $a, b$ and $c$ respectively correspond to :
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/FDNkr1-n1jSa40_tHyMrUO94OSN_TFtIax96QZrxgqU.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{P}$, $\mathrm{M}$ and $\mathrm{D}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{M}$, $\mathrm{D}$ and $\mathrm{P}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{P}$, $\mathrm{D}$ and $\mathrm{M}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{D}$, $\mathrm{M}$ and $\mathrm{P}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{M}$, $\mathrm{D}$ and $\mathrm{P}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

On giving same amount of heat at constant pressure, there is no change in temperature for mono, dia and polyatomic.
$(\Delta \mathrm{Q})_{\mathrm{P}}=\mu \mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{p}} \Delta \mathrm{T}\left(\mu=\frac{\text { No. of molecules }}{\text { Avogedro's no. }}\right)$
or $\quad \Delta \mathrm{T} \propto \frac{1}{\text { no. of molecules }}$

</details>

### Q115. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An ideal monatomic gas with pressure $P$, volume $V$ and temperature $T$ is expanded isothermally to a volume $2 V$ and a final pressure $P_i$. If the same gas is expanded adiabatically to a volume $2 \mathrm{~V}$, the final pressure is $P_a$. The ratio $\frac{P_a}{P_i}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2^{-1 / 3}$
- **(B)** $2^{1 / 3^6}$
- **(C)** $2^{2 / 3}$
- **(D)** $2^{-2 / 3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2^{-2 / 3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For isothermal process :
$$
\begin{aligned}
& P V=P_i 2 V \\
& P=2 P_i
\end{aligned}
$$
For adiabatic process
$$
\mathrm{PV}^\gamma=\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{a}}(2 \mathrm{~V})^\gamma
$$
$(\because$ for monatomic gas $\gamma=5 / 3)$
[From (i) ]
or, $\quad 2 \mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{i}} \mathrm{V}^{\frac{5}{3}}=P_a(2 \mathrm{~V})^{\frac{5}{3}}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow \frac{P_a}{P_i}=\frac{2}{2^{\frac{5}{3}}} \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{P_a}{P_i}=2^{\frac{-2}{3}}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q116. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The pressure of an ideal gas varies with volume as $P=\alpha V$, where $\alpha$ is a constant. One mole of the gas is allowed to undergo expansion such that its volume becomes ' $m$ ' times its initial volume. The work done by the gas in the process is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\alpha V}{2}\left(m^2-1\right)$
- **(B)** $\frac{\alpha^2 V^2}{2}\left(m^2-1\right)$
- **(C)** $\frac{\alpha}{2}\left(m^2-1\right)$
- **(D)** $\frac{\alpha V^2}{2}\left(m^2-1\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{\alpha V^2}{2}\left(m^2-1\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $P=\alpha V$
Work done, $w=\int_V^{m V} P d V$
$=\int_V^{m V} \alpha V d V=\frac{\alpha V^2}{2}\left(m^2-1\right)$.

</details>

### Q117. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

$n$ moles of an ideal gas undergo a process $A \rightarrow B$ as shown in the figure. Maximum temperature of the gas during the process is
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5K-QnjAnePVrTyiX2jsk1Tn3reCBdq9nhssOS9kB51s.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{9 P_0 V_0}{n R}$
- **(B)** $\frac{3 P_0 V_0}{2 n R}$
- **(C)** $\frac{9 P_0 V_0}{2 n R}$
- **(D)** $\frac{9 P_0 V_0}{4 n R}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{9 P_0 V_0}{4 n R}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For given graph, equation of $P$ - $V$ line is
$$
P-2 P_0=\frac{2 P_0-P_0}{V_0-2 V_0}\left(V-V_0\right)
$$
So, $P=3 P_0-\frac{P_0}{V_0} V$ as $P V=n R T$
$$
\Rightarrow\left(3 P_0-\frac{P_0}{V_0} V\right) V=n R T ;
$$
For maximum temperature $\frac{d T}{d V}=0$
$$
\Rightarrow \frac{d T}{d V}=3 P_0-\frac{2 P_0}{V_0} V=0 \Rightarrow V=\frac{3}{2} V_0
$$
Also $P=\frac{3}{2} P_0$
So, $T=\frac{P V}{n R}=\frac{1}{n R} \times \frac{3}{2} P_0 \times \frac{3}{2} V_0=\frac{9 P_0 V_0}{4 n R}$

</details>

### Q118. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A given ideal gas with $\gamma=\frac{C_p}{C_v}=1.5$ at a temperature $T$. If the gas is compressed adiabatically to one-fourth of its initial volume, the final temperature will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 \sqrt{2} T$
- **(B)** $4 T$
- **(C)** $2 T$
- **(D)** $8 T$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2 T$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$T V^{\gamma-1}=$ constant
$$
\begin{aligned}
& T_1 V_1^{\gamma-1}=\mathrm{T}_2 V_2^{\gamma-1} \\
& \Rightarrow T(V)^{1 / 2}=\mathrm{T}_2\left(\frac{V}{4}\right)^{1 / 2} \\
& {\left[\because \gamma=1.5, T_1=T, V_1=V \text { and } V_2=\frac{V}{4}\right]} \\
& \therefore \quad T_2=\left(\frac{4 V}{V}\right)^{1 / 2} T=2 T
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q119. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

This question has Statement 1 and Statement 2. Of the four choices given after the Statements, choose the one that best describes the two Statements.
Statement 1: In an adiabatic process, change in internal energy of a gas is equal to work done on/ by the gas in the process.
Statement 2: The temperature of a gas remains constant in an adiabatic process.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement 1 is true, Statement 2 is true, Statement 2 is a correct explanation of Statement 1.
- **(B)** Statement 1 is true, Statement 2 is false.
- **(C)** Statement 1 is false, Statement 2 is true.
- **(D)** Statement 1 is false, Statement 2 is true, Statement 2 is not a correct explanation of Statement 1.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Statement 1 is true, Statement 2 is false.**

**Detailed Solution:**

In an adiabatic process, $\delta \mathrm{H}=0$ And according to first law of thermodynamics
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \delta H=\delta U+W \\
& \therefore W=-\delta U
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q120. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A perfect gas at $27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ is heated at constant pressure so as to double its volume. The final temperature of the gas will be, close to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $327^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $200^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $54^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $300^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $327^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, $V_1=V$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& V_2=2 V \\
& T_1=27^{\circ}+273=300 \mathrm{~K} \\
& T_2=?
\end{aligned}
$$
From charle's law
$$
\frac{V_1}{T_1}=\frac{V_2}{T_2}(\because \text { Pressure is constant })
$$
or, $\frac{V}{300}=\frac{2 V}{T_2}$

$$
\therefore \quad T_2=600 \mathrm{~K}=600-273=327^{\circ} \mathrm{C}
$$

</details>

### Q121. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Helium gas goes through a cycle $\mathrm{ABCDA}$ (consisting of two isochoric and two isobaric lines) as shown in figure. Efficiency of this cycle is nearly: 
 (Assume the gas to be close to ideal gas)
 <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/rVdThf6O8H2spdWc4Dnsk-1mT0Kk5WwjwRbvAp0K6cY.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $15.4 \%$
- **(B)** $9.1 \%$
- **(C)** $10.5 \%$
- **(D)** $12.5 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $15.4 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done in complete cycle $=$ Area under $\mathrm{P-V}$ graph
$=\mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{V}_0$
from $A$ to $B$, heat given to the gas
$=\mathrm{nC}_{\mathrm{v}} \Delta \mathrm{T}=\mathrm{n} \frac{3}{2} \mathrm{R} \Delta \mathrm{T}=\frac{3}{2} \mathrm{~V}_0 \Delta \mathrm{P}=\frac{3}{2} \mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{V}_0$
from $B$ to $C$, heat given to the system
$=n C_{\mathrm{p}} \Delta \mathrm{T}=\mathrm{n}\left(\frac{5}{2} \mathrm{R}\right) \Delta \mathrm{T}$
$=\frac{5}{2}\left(2 \mathrm{P}_0\right) \Delta \mathrm{V}=5 \mathrm{P}_0 \mathrm{V}_0$
from $C$ to $D$ and $D$ to $A$, heat is rejected.
$\text { efficiency, } \eta=\frac{\text { work done by gas }}{\text { heat given to the gas }} \times 100$
$\eta=\frac{P_0 V_0}{\frac{3}{2} P_0 V_0+5 P_0 V_0}=15.4 \%$

</details>

### Q122. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A diatomic ideal gas is used in a Car engine as the working substance. If during the adiabatic expansion part of the cycle, volume of the gas increases from $\mathrm{V}$ to $32 \mathrm{~V}$ the efficiency of the engine is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.5$
- **(B)** $0.75$
- **(C)** $0.99$
- **(D)** $0.25$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.75$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The efficiency of cycle is
$$
\eta=1-\frac{T_2}{T_1}
$$
for adiabatic process
$$
\text { TV }^{r^{-1}}=\text { constant }
$$
For diatomic gas $\gamma=\frac{7}{5}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{T}_1 \mathrm{~V}_1^{\gamma-1}=\mathrm{T}_2 \mathrm{~V}_2^{\gamma-1} \\
& \mathrm{~T}_1=\mathrm{T}_2\left(\frac{\mathrm{V}_2}{\mathrm{~V}_1}\right)^{\gamma-1} \\
& \mathrm{~T}_1=\mathrm{T}_2(32)^{\frac{7}{5}-1} \\
&=\mathrm{T}_2\left(2^5\right)^{2 / 5} \\
&=\mathrm{T}_2 \times 4 \\
& \mathrm{~T}_1=4 \mathrm{~T}_2 . \\
& \eta=\left(1-\frac{1}{4}\right)=\frac{3}{4}=0.75
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q123. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The net work done on the gas in the cycle ABCDA is

**Options:**

- **(A)** Zero
- **(B)** $276 \mathrm{R}$
- **(C)** $1076 \mathrm{R}$
- **(D)** $1904 \mathrm{R}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $276 \mathrm{R}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
\text { Net work done in a cycle } & =W_{A B}+W_{B C}+W_{C B}+W_{B A} \\
& =400 R+2 \times 2.303 \times 500 R \ln 2-400 R-414 R \\
& =1000 R \times \ln 2-600 R \times \ln 2=400 R \times \ln 2=276 R
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q124. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The work done on the gas in taking it from $D$ to $A$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-414 \mathrm{R}$
- **(B)** $+414 \mathrm{R}$
- **(C)** $-690 \mathrm{R}$
- **(D)** $+690 R$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $-414 \mathrm{R}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

At constant temperature (isothermal process)
$$
\begin{aligned}
W_{D A}=n R T \ln \left(\frac{P_1}{P_2}\right)= & 2.303 \times 2 \mathrm{R} \times 300 \log \left(\frac{10^5}{2 \times 10^5}\right) \\
& =2.303 \times 600 \mathrm{R} \log \left(\frac{1}{2}\right) \\
& =0.693 \times 600 \mathrm{R}=-414 \mathrm{R} .
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q125. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Assuming the gas to be ideal the work done on the gas in taking it from $A$ to $B$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $200 \mathrm{R}$
- **(B)** $300 \mathrm{R}$
- **(C)** $400 \mathrm{R}$
- **(D)** $500 \mathrm{R}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $400 \mathrm{R}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& W_{A B}=\Delta Q-\Delta U={ }^{\prime} C_p d T-n C_v d T \text { (at } \\
& \text { constant pressure }) \\
& =n\left(C_p-C_v\right) d t \\
& =n R d T=2 \times R \times(500-300)=400 \mathrm{R}
\end{aligned}
$$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/D_i9Os7KCdpdayR6IeU5E2c42VbTCObcGBxsjnx4J10.original.fullsize.png">

</details>
